The change is architecturally simple: Someguy implements the identical Delegated Routing V1 HTTP API that `delegated-ipfs.dev` uses (same spec, same wire format), and the existing `DelegatedRoutingClient` already abstracts the routing URL behind the `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` environment variable. The core code change is a URL swap.

.learnings/2026-02-07-atomic-file-upload.md:17:- **IPNS is unreliable for primary data retrieval:** The delegated routing service (`delegated-ipfs.dev`) returns "routing: not found" for IPNS lookups. IPNS records are ephemeral (~48h TTL) and the public DHT doesn't reliably index them. The entire file browsing experience breaks on page reload because folder metadata can't be resolved. The server already stores `metadata_cid` in `folder_ipns` table on publish - this should be the primary lookup source with IPNS as secondary.

     1→import { Injectable, HttpException, HttpStatus, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';
     3→
     4→/** Typed error for non-retryable HTTP responses from the delegated routing API. */
     5→class DelegatedRoutingHttpError extends Error {
     6→  constructor(public readonly status: number) {
     7→    super(`Delegated routing returned ${status}`);
     8→    this.name = 'DelegatedRoutingHttpError';
     9→  }
    10→}
    11→
    12→@Injectable()
    13→export class DelegatedRoutingClient {
    14→  private readonly logger = new Logger(DelegatedRoutingClient.name);
    15→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    16→  private readonly maxRetries = 3;
    17→  private readonly baseDelayMs = 1000;
    18→  private readonly maxRetryDelayMs = 30_000;
    19→  private readonly requestTimeoutMs = 10_000;
    20→
    21→  constructor(private readonly configService: ConfigService) {
    22→    this.delegatedRoutingUrl = this.configService.get<string>(
    23→      'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',
    24→      'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'
    25→    );
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  /**
    29→   * Publish an IPNS record via the delegated routing PUT API with exponential backoff retry.
    30→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on persistent failures.
    31→   */
    32→  async publish(ipnsName: string, recordBytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void> {
    33→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
    34→
    35→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
    36→
    37→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
    38→      try {
    39→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
    40→          url,
    41→          {
    42→            method: 'PUT',
    43→            headers: {
    44→              'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
    45→            },
    46→            body: recordBytes as unknown as BodyInit,
    47→          },
    48→          async (response) => ({
    49→            ok: response.ok,
    50→            status: response.status,
    51→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
    52→            errorText: !response.ok && response.status !== 429 ? await response.text() : '',
    53→          })
    54→        );
    55→
    56→        if (ok) {
    57→          this.logger.log(`IPNS record published successfully for ${ipnsName}`);
    58→          return;
    59→        }
    60→
    61→        // Handle rate limiting
    62→        if (status === 429) {
    63→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    64→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
    65→
    66→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS publish, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
    67→            await this.delay(delayMs);
    68→          }
    69→          continue;
    70→        }
    71→
    72→        // Non-retryable HTTP error
    73→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
    74→        this.logger.error(`Delegated routing returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`);
    75→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
    76→      } catch (error) {
    77→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
    78→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
    79→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
    80→          throw new HttpException(
    81→            'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network',
    82→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
    83→          );
    84→        }
    85→
    86→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
    87→
    88→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
    89→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    90→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
    91→          this.logger.warn(
    92→            `IPNS publish attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
    93→          );
    94→          await this.delay(delayMs);
    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→    }
    98→
    99→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   100→    this.logger.error(
   101→      `Failed to publish IPNS record after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   102→    );
   103→    throw new HttpException(
   104→      'Failed to publish IPNS record to routing network after multiple attempts',
   105→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   106→    );
   107→  }
   108→
   109→  /**
   110→   * Resolve an IPNS name via the delegated routing GET API with retries.
   111→   * Returns the raw record bytes, or null if the IPNS name is not found (404).
   112→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on routing failures.
   113→   */
   114→  async resolve(ipnsName: string): Promise<Uint8Array | null> {
   115→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
   116→
   117→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
   118→
   119→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
   120→      try {
   121→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, data, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
   122→          url,
   123→          {
   124→            method: 'GET',
   125→            headers: {
   126→              Accept: 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
   127→            },
   128→          },
   129→          async (response) => ({
   130→            ok: response.ok,
   131→            status: response.status,
   132→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
   133→            data: response.ok ? new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()) : null,
   134→            errorText:
   135→              !response.ok && response.status !== 404 && response.status !== 429
   136→                ? await response.text()
   137→                : '',
   138→          })
   139→        );
   140→
   141→        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error
   142→        if (status === 404) {
   143→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found: ${ipnsName}`);
   144→          return null;
   145→        }
   146→
   147→        if (ok) {
   148→          return data;
   149→        }
   150→
   151→        // Handle rate limiting
   152→        if (status === 429) {
   153→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   154→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
   155→
   156→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS resolve, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   157→            await this.delay(delayMs);
   158→          }
   159→          continue;
   160→        }
   161→
   162→        // Non-retryable error
   163→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   164→        this.logger.error(
   165→          `Delegated routing resolution returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   166→        );
   167→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
   168→      } catch (error) {
   169→        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry
   170→        if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   171→          throw error;
   172→        }
   173→
   174→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
   175→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
   176→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   177→          throw new HttpException(
   178→            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',
   179→            HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   180→          );
   181→        }
   182→
   183→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   184→
   185→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   186→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   187→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   188→          this.logger.warn(
   189→            `IPNS resolve attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying in ${delayMs}ms: ${lastError.message}`
   190→          );
   191→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   192→        }
   193→      }
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   197→    this.logger.error(
   198→      `Failed to resolve IPNS name after ${this.maxRetries} attempts: ${lastError?.message}`
   199→    );
   200→    throw new HttpException(
   201→      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',
   202→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   203→    );
   204→  }
   205→
   206→  private async fetchWithTimeout<T>(
   207→    url: string,
   208→    init: RequestInit,
   209→    consume: (response: Response) => Promise<T>
   210→  ): Promise<T> {
   211→    const controller = new AbortController();
   212→    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), this.requestTimeoutMs);
   213→    try {
   214→      const response = await fetch(url, { ...init, signal: controller.signal });
   215→      return await consume(response);
   216→    } finally {
   217→      clearTimeout(timeout);
   218→    }
   219→  }
   220→
   221→  private getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter: string | null, attempt: number): number {
   222→    const fallback = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   223→    if (!retryAfter) return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   224→
   225→    const seconds = Number(retryAfter);
   226→    if (Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds >= 0) {
   227→      return Math.min(seconds * 1000, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    const retryAt = Date.parse(retryAfter);
   231→    if (!Number.isNaN(retryAt)) {
   232→      return Math.min(Math.max(0, retryAt - Date.now()), this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   233→    }
   234→
   235→    return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   236→  }
   237→
   238→  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
   239→    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→

   140→## Environment Configuration
   141→
   142→**API (`apps/api/.env.example`):**
   143→
   144→- `DB_HOST`, `DB_PORT`, `DB_USERNAME`, `DB_PASSWORD`, `DB_DATABASE` - PostgreSQL connection
   145→- `REDIS_HOST`, `REDIS_PORT` - Redis connection (for BullMQ)
   146→- `IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL`, `IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL` - IPFS Kubo daemon endpoints
   147→- `JWT_SECRET` - Access token signing
   148→- `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` - Allowed origins (comma-separated, supports wildcards)
   149→- `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` - IPNS delegated routing endpoint
   150→- `TEE_WORKER_URL`, `TEE_WORKER_SECRET` - TEE worker connection
   151→- `SENDGRID_API_KEY`, `SENDGRID_FROM_EMAIL` - Email OTP delivery
   152→
   153→**Web (`apps/web/.env.example`):**

    55→**Recommendation: Use the local Kubo node as the primary IPNS routing endpoint.**
    56→
    57→Since Kubo v0.23.0, the `Gateway.ExposeRoutingAPI` config option exposes the standard Delegated Routing V1 HTTP API at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/routing/v1`. This means the `DelegatedRoutingClient` can be pointed at the local Kubo gateway instead of the public internet, with zero code changes to its HTTP client logic (same API contract: `GET /routing/v1/ipns/{name}`, `PUT /routing/v1/ipns/{name}`).
    58→
    59→**Resolution path:** Local Kubo DHT lookup (primary) -> DB-cached CID (fallback, already implemented) -> `delegated-ipfs.dev` (tertiary fallback, degraded mode only).
    60→
    61→### Configuration Changes Required
    62→
    63→| What                                   | Current                                | New                                                 |
    64→| -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
    65→| `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` env var        | `https://delegated-ipfs.dev`           | `http://localhost:8080`                             |
    66→| Kubo config `Gateway.ExposeRoutingAPI` | Not set (defaults to `false` on v0.34) | Set to `true`                                       |
    67→| Docker compose `ipfs` service          | No config volume mount                 | Mount custom config or use `ipfs config` init       |
    68→| Fallback routing                       | None (single source + DB cache)        | Chain: local Kubo -> DB cache -> delegated-ipfs.dev |
    69→
    70→### Optional: Upgrade Kubo to v0.40.1
    71→
    72→| Benefit                                         | Kubo v0.34.0  | Kubo v0.40.1                         |
    73→| ----------------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------ |
    74→| `Gateway.ExposeRoutingAPI` available            | Yes (opt-in)  | Yes (default on)                     |
```

     1→# Environment Architecture
     2→
     3→**Created:** 2026-01-25
     4→
     5→## Overview
     6→
     7→CipherBox requires isolated environments to prevent cross-environment interference, particularly with IPNS sequence numbers. This document defines the environment architecture and solves the Web3Auth key isolation problem.
     8→
     9→## The Problem
    10→
    11→**Current State:** All environments share the same Web3Auth project (SAPPHIRE_DEVNET) and client ID. This means:
    12→
    13→- Same user identity → same derived secp256k1 keypair → same Ed25519 IPNS keypair
    14→- IPNS records use monotonically increasing sequence numbers
    15→- If CI publishes seq=100, local dev (fresh DB) tries seq=1 → rejected by network/delegated routing
    16→- Test repeatability is impossible without manual intervention
    17→
    18→## Environment Matrix
    19→
    20→| Environment    | IPFS Mode      | Web3Auth Network | IPNS Routing           | Database            | TEE Republishing     | Isolation Level            |
    21→| -------------- | -------------- | ---------------- | ---------------------- | ------------------- | -------------------- | -------------------------- |
    22→| **Local Dev**  | Kubo (offline) | Sapphire Devnet  | Mock service           | Local Postgres      | **Disabled**         | Full                       |
    23→| **CI E2E**     | Kubo (offline) | Sapphire Devnet  | Mock service (per-run) | Ephemeral Postgres  | **Disabled**         | Full per-run               |
    24→| **Staging**    | Kubo (online)  | Sapphire Devnet  | delegated-ipfs.dev     | Managed Postgres    | **Active** (testnet) | Shared with Local/CI users |
    25→| **Production** | Kubo (managed) | Sapphire Mainnet | delegated-ipfs.dev     | Production Postgres | **Active** (mainnet) | Full                       |
    26→
    27→## Solution: Environment-Aware Key Derivation
    28→
    29→### Recommended Approach: Environment-Specific IPNS Key Derivation
    30→
```

.learnings/2026-02-07-atomic-file-upload.md:17:- **IPNS is unreliable for primary data retrieval:** The delegated routing service (`delegated-ipfs.dev`) returns "routing: not found" for IPNS lookups. IPNS records are ephemeral (~48h TTL) and the public DHT doesn't reliably index them. The entire file browsing experience breaks on page reload because folder metadata can't be resolved. The server already stores `metadata_cid` in `folder_ipns` table on publish - this should be the primary lookup source with IPNS as secondary.
.learnings/2026-02-25-ipns-stale-resolution-staging.md:11:- **delegated-ipfs.dev serves stale IPNS records**: The network resolver caches records with a TTL that can be minutes behind the latest publish. This is invisible locally (same-machine IPFS resolves instantly) but causes consistent failures on staging.

    54→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:43:**Key problem:** `delegated-ipfs.dev` is the sole IPNS resolution path from the API. The external service adds latency (10s timeout, 3 retries with backoff) and has documented 502 reliability issues.
    55→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:79:## 2. IPNS Resolution: Replace delegated-ipfs.dev
    56→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:83:**Strategy:** Invert the current resolution priority. Make the DB-cached CID the primary resolution source (it is already written synchronously on every publish). Use the self-hosted Kubo node's `/api/v0/name/resolve` endpoint for background DHT verification. Demote `delegated-ipfs.dev` to a disabled-by-default fallback.
    57→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:87:1. **Eliminates external dependency.** The self-hosted Kubo node already participates in the IPFS DHT. It can resolve IPNS names directly without delegated-ipfs.dev.
    58→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:98:  -> DelegatedRoutingClient.resolve() -> https://delegated-ipfs.dev (primary, 10s timeout)
    59→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:178:2. Broadcasts the pre-signed record to the DHT via Kubo's `/api/v0/routing/put` (replaces delegated-ipfs.dev)
    60→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:179:3. Falls back to delegated-ipfs.dev only if Kubo routing put fails and the fallback is enabled
    61→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:181:**For TEE republishes:** The existing flow is preserved: the TEE decrypts the IPNS private key in hardware, signs the IPNS record inside the TEE boundary, and returns the **signed record bytes** (not the raw key) to the API. The API then broadcasts the pre-signed record to the DHT via Kubo's `/api/v0/routing/put`, replacing delegated-ipfs.dev as the broadcast target.
    62→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:206:The recovery tool (`apps/web/public/recovery.html`) currently resolves IPNS via `delegated-ipfs.dev` directly from the browser (no API needed -- the tool works offline from the CipherBox API).
    63→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:211:2. Fall back to `delegated-ipfs.dev` only if the API is unreachable (true offline recovery)
    64→.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md:770:                 -> DelegatedRoutingClient.publish() -> delegated-ipfs.dev

    72→.planning/research/STACK.md:49:## Feature 1: IPNS Reliability (Replace delegated-ipfs.dev)
    73→.planning/research/STACK.md:53:The current system publishes and resolves IPNS records via `delegated-ipfs.dev`, a public delegated routing endpoint. This endpoint is unreliable (known 502 errors). However, the self-hosted Kubo v0.34.0 node **already participates in the Amino DHT** and can resolve IPNS records locally via its RPC API or via the Routing V1 HTTP API.

    77→.planning/research/STACK.md:83:[Someguy](https://github.com/ipfs/someguy) (v0.11.1, Feb 2026) is the software that powers `delegated-ipfs.dev`. It could be self-hosted as a Docker container (`ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1`) alongside Kubo.
    78→.planning/research/STACK.md:85:**Why NOT Someguy:** Someguy is a proxy that forwards requests to the DHT via a Kubo-like client. Running Someguy next to Kubo adds a middleman with no benefit -- Kubo already does DHT lookups directly. Someguy makes sense for serving many light clients at scale (the `delegated-ipfs.dev` use case), not for a single application with its own Kubo node.

```
   148→- Why fragile: Vendored fork of fuser 0.16 with socket-read patch for FUSE-T compatibility. Upstream updates cannot be trivially merged. The patch is load-bearing -- without it, large file writes crash the FUSE session.
   149→- Safe modification: Never update without re-applying the `channel.rs` receive() patch. Document patch diff in vendor directory.
   150→- Test coverage: No tests for the patched receive() function.
   151→
   152→**Delegated routing (delegated-ipfs.dev) dependency:**
   153→
   154→- Files: `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts`
   155→- Why fragile: The external service at `delegated-ipfs.dev` has been unreliable historically (502 errors documented in memory). It is the sole path for IPNS record publishing and resolution from the API.
   156→- Safe modification: The client has retry with exponential backoff (3 retries, 1s base delay, 30s cap). Changes to the API or rate limits could break publishing.
   157→- Test coverage: Unit tests at `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.spec.ts` cover retry logic. No integration tests against real service.
   158→
   159→**Web3Auth MPC Core Kit integration:**
   160→
   161→- Files: `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/core-kit.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/hooks.ts` (multiple `as any` casts), `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts` (522 lines)
   162→- Why fragile: Web3Auth SDK has poor TypeScript definitions (`any` casts required). The `REQUIRED_SHARE` state handling at `hooks.ts:205-226` works around a bug where Web3Auth doesn't auto-check localStorage for device factors. SDK version upgrades frequently change behavior.
   163→- Safe modification: Test all auth flows (email, Google, wallet) end-to-end after any Web3Auth dependency update.
   164→- Test coverage: Auth flow tested via E2E (`tests/e2e/tests/full-workflow.spec.ts`) but Web3Auth unit mocking is complex.
   165→
   166→## Scaling Limits
   167→
   168→**IPNS record propagation and TEE republishing:**
   169→
   170→- Current capacity: TEE republishes all enrolled IPNS records every 6 hours via batch endpoint.
   171→- Limit: At 1000+ enrolled records per user, republish cycles may exceed the 3-hour window. The `delete.service.ts:28` documents this threshold.
   172→- Scaling path: Implement IPNS unenrollment on deletion (see Tech Debt section). Consider per-user republish prioritization.
   173→
   174→**Folder metadata size (1000 files per folder):**
   175→
   176→- Current capacity: PRD constrains to 1000 children per folder.
   177→- Limit: Metadata blob grows linearly with children count. With FilePointers (~100 bytes each), a 1000-file folder produces ~100 KB of metadata before encryption.
   178→- Scaling path: This limit is enforced by design. For larger collections, users must create subfolders.
   179→
   180→**File size limit (100 MB with GCM):**
   181→
   182→- Current capacity: 100 MB per file per PRD constraint.
   183→- Limit: Browser memory pressure with full-file buffering for GCM encryption.
   184→- Scaling path: CTR streaming encryption is implemented but not yet the default for uploads. Switching to CTR for large files would remove the memory constraint.
   185→
   186→**Single Kubo IPFS node:**
   187→
   188→- Current capacity: One Kubo node handles all pinning/unpinning for the entire deployment.
   189→- Limit: Single point of failure. Kubo node downtime = no uploads or downloads.
   190→- Scaling path: Add IPFS cluster for redundancy, or migrate to a managed IPFS pinning service (Pinata, web3.storage).
   191→
   192→## Dependencies at Risk
   193→
   194→**delegated-ipfs.dev (external service):**
   195→
   196→- Risk: Third-party service with documented unreliability. No SLA. Single point of failure for IPNS operations.
   197→- Impact: If down, no IPNS records can be published or resolved. File metadata becomes temporarily inaccessible.
   198→- Migration plan: DB-cached CID fallback exists for resolution. Consider self-hosting a delegated routing endpoint or adding a secondary provider.
   199→
   200→**Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (@web3auth/mpc-core-kit@^3.5.0):**
   201→
   202→- Risk: Complex SDK with frequent breaking changes. Poor TypeScript types require `any` casts. Authentication is entirely dependent on Web3Auth infrastructure.
```

    58→  -> folder_ipns DB query (fallback on 502/timeout)

    64→```text

id: toolu_01GjtGwfhKfr51bYmTZZdbWj
```
    62→### v1.1 flow (IPNS-02)
    63→
    64→```text
    65→resolveRecord()
    66→  -> folder_ipns DB query (primary, <5ms)
    67→  -> Return DB result immediately to caller
    68→  -> Async: Kubo DHT resolve via self-hosted Someguy (background)
    69→     -> If DHT has higher sequence number, update DB cache
    70→     -> If DHT fails, no-op (DB is authoritative for our own records)
    71→```
    72→
    73→### Why DB-first instead of DHT-first
    74→
    75→1. **Latency:** DB query completes in <5ms. DHT resolution, even self-hosted, has a median of 0.3-0.4s (ProbeLab data) and a long tail. Users should never wait for the DHT on the hot path.
    76→2. **DB is already correct for our own records.** Every publish writes to `folder_ipns` atomically. The DB always has the latest CID that _we_ published. The only scenario where DHT has a fresher value is if another node published for the same IPNS name — which doesn't happen in CipherBox's architecture.
```

    48→
    49→### Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement
    50→
    51→**Goal**: Users experience reliable, fast IPNS resolution without dependency on external delegated-ipfs.dev service
    52→**Depends on**: Phase 18 (baselines must exist to measure improvement)
    53→**Requirements**: IPNS-01, IPNS-02, IPNS-03, IPNS-04
    54→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    55→
    56→1. Self-hosted Someguy is deployed alongside Kubo and serves as the IPNS routing provider, with delegated-ipfs.dev fully removed from the resolution path
    57→2. IPNS resolution completes within 2 seconds in the normal case (DB-first with async DHT verification) and degrades gracefully to DB-only when DHT is slow
    58→3. The standalone recovery tool resolves IPNS records via self-hosted Someguy without depending on the CipherBox API or delegated-ipfs.dev
    59→4. No user-visible errors or stale metadata when the DHT is temporarily unreachable (DB fallback serves correct data)
    60→   **Plans**: 2 plans
    61→
    62→Plans:
    63→
    64→- [ ] 19-01-PLAN.md -- Deploy Someguy Docker sidecar, update routing URL in deploy workflow and .env.example
    65→- [ ] 19-02-PLAN.md -- Add IPNS resolve/publish latency histograms and instrument IpnsService with timing
    66→
    67→### Phase 20: Vault Migration
    68→
    69→**Goal**: The server stores zero crypto material -- rootFolderKey lives in the IPFS vault blob, making the server a true zero-knowledge relay
    70→**Depends on**: Phase 19 (IPNS must be reliable before making it a login-adjacent dependency)
    71→**Requirements**: VAULT-01, VAULT-02, VAULT-03, VAULT-04, VAULT-05, VAULT-06
    72→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    73→
    74→1. New users get vault blob v2 format on first login, with rootFolderKey ECIES-wrapped in the blob header and readable without any database round-trip
    75→2. Existing users are lazily migrated to vault blob v2 on their next folder metadata publish, with the DB copy retained as a permanent fallback
    76→3. The standalone recovery tool parses vault blob v2 and extracts rootFolderKey without needing the CipherBox API or database
    77→4. The desktop app (Rust) parses vault blob v2 and uses the embedded rootFolderKey for FUSE mount initialization
    78→5. The encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey column is deprecated from the vaults table (HKDF derivation is the canonical path)
    79→   **Plans**: TBD
    80→
    81→### Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support
    82→
    83→**Goal**: Users can configure their own IPFS node for data sovereignty, with files pinned to both CipherBox and their personal node
    84→**Depends on**: Phase 19 (stable IPNS resolution benefits BYO workflows)
    85→**Requirements**: BYO-01, BYO-02, BYO-03, BYO-04, BYO-05, BYO-06, BYO-07
    86→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    87→
    88→1. A user can enter their IPFS node endpoint and credentials in Settings, test the connection, and see a success/failure result
    89→2. After configuring a BYO node, every file upload is pinned to both the CipherBox node (always) and the user's node (best-effort mirror)
    90→3. All IPNS publishes still route through the CipherBox API regardless of BYO configuration (no bypass of optimistic concurrency)
    91→4. BYO users see an advisory quota display (not enforced) with clear indication that storage is managed by their own node
    92→5. The connection test endpoint validates reachability and API compatibility of the user's node before saving configuration
    93→   **Plans**: TBD
    94→
    95→### Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion
    96→
    97→**Goal**: Complete performance picture exists -- client-side timing, end-to-end journeys, load test results, and capacity recommendations are documented
    98→**Depends on**: Phase 21 (all features must be stable to produce meaningful baselines)
    99→**Requirements**: PERF-05, PERF-06, PERF-07, PERF-08
   100→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
   101→
   102→1. Client-side timing instrumentation captures encrypt/decrypt throughput, upload/download duration, and IPNS operation timing in the browser
   103→2. End-to-end user journey timings are captured for login-to-vault, upload-to-visible, and share-to-accessible workflows
   104→3. k6 load test scripts simulate concurrent users performing upload, download, publish, and resolve operations with documented pass/fail thresholds
   105→4. Capacity thresholds are documented with scaling recommendations (max concurrent users, storage growth projections, IPNS publish throughput limits)

    60→        throw new BadRequestException('Invalid base64-encoded record');
    61→      }
    62→
    63→      // Save to DB first so resolve always has a fallback, even if delegated
    64→      // routing fails (e.g. rate-limited, network error, DHT propagation delay).
    65→      const folder = await this.upsertFolderIpns(
    66→        userId,
    67→        dto.ipnsName,
    68→        dto.metadataCid,
    69→        dto.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
    70→        dto.keyEpoch,
    71→        recordType,
    72→        dto.expectedSequenceNumber
    73→      );
    74→
    75→      // Publish to delegated routing API (best-effort — DB is the reliable source)
    76→      const publishStart = process.hrtime.bigint();
    77→      let publishOutcome = 'success';
    78→      try {
    79→        await this.delegatedRouting.publish(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes);
    80→      } catch (error) {
    81→        result = 'delegated_error';
    82→        publishOutcome =
    83→          error instanceof Error && error.name === 'AbortError' ? 'timeout' : 'error';
    84→        this.logger.warn(
    85→          `Delegated routing publish failed for ${dto.ipnsName}, DB record saved: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
    86→        );
    87→      } finally {
    88→        const publishElapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - publishStart) / 1e9;
    89→        this.metricsService.ipnsPublishDuration.observe(
    90→          { outcome: publishOutcome },
    91→          publishElapsed
    92→        );
    93→      }
    94→
    95→      return {
    96→        success: true,
    97→        ipnsName: dto.ipnsName,
    98→        sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
    99→      };

{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2899733453","pull_request_review_id":3908793418,"id":2899733453,"node_id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6s1mvN","diff_hunk":"@@ -0,0 +1,489 @@\n+# Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement - Research\n+\n+**Researched:** 2026-03-07\n+**Domain:** IPFS Delegated Routing, Docker Compose, Prometheus Metrics\n+**Confidence:** HIGH\n+\n+## Summary\n+\n+Phase 19 replaces the external `delegated-ipfs.dev` delegated routing service with a self-hosted Someguy instance running as a Docker Compose sidecar. The change is architecturally simple: Someguy implements the identical Delegated Routing V1 HTTP API that `delegated-ipfs.dev` uses (same spec, same wire format), and the existing `DelegatedRoutingClient` already abstracts the routing URL behind the `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` environment variable. The core code change is a URL swap.\n+\n+Someguy v0.11.1 (released February 2026) runs its own embedded libp2p node with a built-in DHT client -- it does NOT require Kubo for DHT connectivity. It listens on `127.0.0.1:8190` by default and exposes the standard `/routing/v1/ipns/{name}` endpoints (GET for resolve, PUT for publish). The Docker image is `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1` and runs with entrypoint `someguy start`. It has no `/health` endpoint -- health checks must use `/version` or TCP port checks.\n+\n+The key risk is resource consumption: Someguy's default `accelerated` DHT mode crawls the entire Amino DHT on startup (12+ hours to warm up, 30k+ peers). For the staging environment, use `SOMEGUY_DHT=standard` to avoid overwhelming a small VPS. The `standard` mode participates in DHT passively (queries on demand, no full crawl).\n+\n+**Primary recommendation:** Deploy Someguy v0.11.1 as a Docker Compose sidecar with `SOMEGUY_DHT=standard` for staging. Change `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` from `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` to `http://someguy:8190`. Add Prometheus histograms for Someguy-specific latency tracking. Keep timeout/retry config identical to Phase 18 baselines.\n+\n+<user_constraints>\n+\n+## User Constraints (from CONTEXT.md)\n+\n+### Locked Decisions\n+\n+- Keep **network-first resolution** (current logic) -- Someguy replaces delegated-ipfs.dev as the routing provider, DB remains the fallback\n+- **No resolution logic change needed** -- just swap `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` from `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` to `http://someguy:<port>`\n+- Keep **identical timeout/retry config** (10s timeout, 3 retries, exponential backoff) for apples-to-apples comparison with Phase 18 delegated-ipfs.dev baselines\n+- **Someguy handles both publish and resolve** for the CipherBox API -- single routing provider, single config\n+- **Docker Compose sidecar** -- new `someguy` service in `docker-compose.staging.yml`, depends on `ipfs` (Kubo)\n+- **Configurable Someguy backend** via environment: Staging: Kubo-only (local DHT, no external HTTP dependencies); Production: Kubo + public Amino DHT (broader resolution coverage)\n+- **TEE worker stays on delegated-ipfs.dev** -- async batch republishes are latency-tolerant\n+- **Recovery defaults to public routing** (delegated-ipfs.dev or public IPFS gateways) for self-sovereignty. Self-hosted Someguy is an optional configurable endpoint, not the default\n+- **Sequential with timeout** (current pattern) -- try Someguy, fall back to DB on failure/timeout. No parallel race\n+- **Dedicated Prometheus metrics** for Someguy resolution: latency histogram, success/db-fallback/timeout counters\n+- **Transparent to client** -- no `source` field in resolve API response. Source tracking is server-side metrics only\n+- **Metrics only, no alerting thresholds** -- establish baselines first, configure alerts after data exists\n+\n+### Claude's Discretion\n+\n+- Exact Someguy Docker image version and configuration flags\n+- Someguy port selection and health check configuration\n+- How to structure the Prometheus histogram labels for Someguy metrics\n+- E2E test updates (mock-ipns-routing may need adjustment)\n+- Whether to update OpenAPI descriptions that reference \"delegated-ipfs.dev\"\n+\n+### Deferred Ideas (OUT OF SCOPE)\n+\n+- **Standalone CLI recovery tool** -- build a Node.js CLI that reads vault export JSON, resolves IPNS, and decrypts/downloads all files. Separate phase.\n+- **TEE worker routing through Someguy** -- revisit when capacity metrics show delegated-ipfs.dev is a bottleneck for batch republishes, or when TEE moves to Phala infra.\n+- **Timeout tuning for UX** -- after baseline data from Phase 18 + early Phase 19, tune timeout/retry for sub-2s user experience.\n+- **Alerting thresholds** -- configure Grafana alerts for DB fallback rate after baseline data exists.\n+\n+</user_constraints>\n+\n+<phase_requirements>\n+\n+## Phase Requirements\n+\n+| ID      | Description                                                                                                       | Research Support                                                                                                                                                       |\n+| ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| IPNS-01 | Self-hosted Someguy deployed alongside Kubo, replacing delegated-ipfs.dev as primary IPNS routing provider        | Someguy v0.11.1 Docker image available at `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1`. Implements same Routing V1 HTTP API. Runs own libp2p+DHT. Config via env vars documented.   |\n+| IPNS-02 | IPNS resolution uses DB-first strategy with async Kubo DHT verification via self-hosted Someguy                   | Per CONTEXT.md, resolution stays **network-first** (not DB-first as REQUIREMENTS.md states). Swap URL only. `IpnsService.resolveRecord()` logic unchanged.             |\n+| IPNS-03 | Recovery tool resolves IPNS via self-hosted Someguy instead of delegated-ipfs.dev                                 | Per CONTEXT.md, recovery defaults to **public routing** for self-sovereignty. Someguy is optional/configurable. No standalone CLI tool this phase.                      |\n+| IPNS-04 | System degrades gracefully when DHT resolution is slow (timeout + DB fallback within 2s)                          | Current sequential timeout+DB fallback pattern preserved. 10s timeout kept for baseline comparison. `DelegatedRoutingClient` already handles timeout and BAD_GATEWAY.  |\n+\n+**Note:** IPNS-02 and IPNS-03 requirement text as written in REQUIREMENTS.md does not match the locked decisions from CONTEXT.md. The CONTEXT.md decisions take precedence. The planner should note these discrepancies but implement per CONTEXT.md.\n+\n+</phase_requirements>\n+\n+## Standard Stack\n+\n+### Core\n+\n+| Library/Tool         | Version  | Purpose                           | Why Standard                                                       |\n+| -------------------- | -------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n+| Someguy              | v0.11.1  | Self-hosted delegated routing     | Same software that powers delegated-ipfs.dev. Stable, maintained.  |\n+| `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy` | v0.11.1 | Docker image for Someguy         | Official GHCR image. Tags: `latest`, `vX.Y.Z`, `main-latest`.     |\n+| `prom-client`        | ^15.1.3  | Prometheus metrics (already used) | Already installed, existing `MetricsService` pattern established.  |\n+\n+### Supporting\n+\n+| Library/Tool       | Version   | Purpose                        | When to Use                                               |\n+| ------------------ | --------- | ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| Kubo               | v0.34.0   | IPFS node (already deployed)   | Someguy uses its own DHT, not Kubo's. Kubo stays as-is.  |\n+| Grafana Alloy      | v1.6.1    | Metrics scraping (already deployed) | May optionally scrape Someguy's `/debug/metrics/prometheus` |\n+\n+### Alternatives Considered\n+\n+| Instead of         | Could Use                    | Tradeoff                                                                                           |\n+| ------------------ | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| Someguy sidecar    | Kubo `Gateway.ExposeRoutingAPI` | Simpler (no new container), but Kubo Routing API on v0.34.0 is opt-in and less feature-rich. User decided Someguy. |\n+| Someguy sidecar    | Direct Kubo RPC (`/api/v0/name/resolve`) | Different API contract, would require rewriting `DelegatedRoutingClient`. Not compatible with current abstraction. |\n+\n+**Installation:** No npm dependencies needed. Docker image pull only:\n+```bash\n+docker pull ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1\n+```\n+\n+## Architecture Patterns\n+\n+### Recommended Project Structure\n+\n+No new source directories needed. Changes are distributed across existing files:\n+\n+```\n+apps/api/src/\n+  ipns/\n+    delegated-routing.client.ts  # No code changes (URL comes from env)\n+    ipns.service.ts              # No code changes (resolution logic unchanged)\n+  metrics/\n+    metrics.service.ts           # Add Someguy-specific histograms\n+docker/\n+  docker-compose.staging.yml     # Add someguy service\n+  alloy-config.river             # Optionally scrape Someguy metrics\n+.github/workflows/\n+  deploy-staging.yml             # Update DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL env var\n+  e2e.yml                        # E2E still uses mock-ipns-routing (no change)\n+apps/api/\n+  .env.example                   # Update docs for DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL\n+```\n+\n+### Pattern 1: Docker Compose Sidecar\n+\n+**What:** Add `someguy` as a service in `docker-compose.staging.yml` that runs alongside the existing `ipfs` (Kubo) service.\n+\n+**When to use:** When a service needs to be co-located and networked with existing services but runs as a separate process.\n+\n+**Example:**\n+```yaml\n+# Source: Someguy docs + existing docker-compose.staging.yml patterns\n+someguy:\n+  image: ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1\n+  restart: unless-stopped\n+  environment:\n+    SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS: 0.0.0.0:8190\n+    SOMEGUY_DHT: standard\n+    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW: 50\n+    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH: 300\n+    SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY: 512MB\n+    GOLOG_LOG_LEVEL: info\n+  logging: *default-logging\n+  healthcheck:\n+    test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'wget -qO- http://localhost:8190/version || exit 1']\n+    interval: 10s\n+    timeout: 5s\n+    retries: 10\n+    start_period: 30s\n+  deploy:\n+    resources:\n+      limits:\n+        memory: 768M\n+        cpus: '0.5'\n+```\n+\n+**Key details:**\n+- `SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS` must be `0.0.0.0:8190` (not default `127.0.0.1:8190`) for Docker inter-container networking\n+- `SOMEGUY_DHT=standard` for staging (avoids 2GB+ memory of accelerated mode)\n+- No exposed host ports needed -- only accessed by `api` service within Docker network\n+- Health check uses `/version` endpoint (Someguy has no `/health` endpoint)\n+- The `api` service accesses Someguy at `http://someguy:8190` via Docker DNS\n+\n+### Pattern 2: Environment Variable URL Swap\n+\n+**What:** Change `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` from the external service to the internal Docker service name.\n+\n+**When to use:** When swapping an external dependency with an internal one that implements the same API contract.\n+\n+**Example in deploy-staging.yml:**\n+```yaml\n+# Before:\n+DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=https://delegated-ipfs.dev\n+# After:\n+DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://someguy:8190\n+```\n+\n+**Key details:**\n+- The `DelegatedRoutingClient` reads this URL at constructor time via `ConfigService`\n+- The URL path `/routing/v1/ipns/{name}` is appended by the client, not included in the env var\n+- E2E tests continue using `http://localhost:3001` (the mock service) -- no E2E change needed\n+- Local development can continue using `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` or the mock service\n+\n+### Pattern 3: Prometheus Histogram for Operation Latency\n+\n+**What:** Add dedicated histograms to track Someguy resolution/publish latency and outcome.\n+\n+**When to use:** When you need p50/p95/p99 latency data for a specific operation, not just success/failure counts.\n+\n+**Example:**\n+```typescript\n+// Source: existing MetricsService patterns in metrics.service.ts\n+// New histograms to add alongside existing counters\n+readonly ipnsPublishDuration: client.Histogram;\n+readonly ipnsResolveDuration: client.Histogram;\n+\n+// In constructor:\n+this.ipnsPublishDuration = new client.Histogram({\n+  name: 'cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds',\n+  help: 'IPNS publish duration in seconds',\n+  labelNames: ['outcome'],  // 'success', 'error', 'timeout'\n+  buckets: [0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60],\n+  registers: [this.registry],\n+});\n+\n+this.ipnsResolveDuration = new client.Histogram({\n+  name: 'cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds',\n+  help: 'IPNS resolve duration in seconds',\n+  labelNames: ['source'],  // 'network', 'db_cache', 'network_stale'\n+  buckets: [0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30],\n+  registers: [this.registry],\n+});\n+```\n+\n+**Label design:**\n+- `ipnsPublishDuration` labels: `outcome` = `success` | `error` | `timeout`\n+- `ipnsResolveDuration` labels: `source` = `network` (Someguy returned result) | `db_cache` (fell back to DB) | `network_stale` (network returned but DB was newer)\n+- Keep cardinality low -- no per-ipnsName labels\n+\n+### Anti-Patterns to Avoid\n+\n+- **Setting `SOMEGUY_DHT=accelerated` in staging:** The accelerated DHT client crawls the entire Amino DHT (30k+ peers, 12+ hours warm-up, 2GB+ memory). Use `standard` for staging, reserve `accelerated` for production if needed.\n+- **Exposing Someguy ports to the host:** Someguy only needs to be reachable from the `api` container. Exposing `8190` to the host is unnecessary and increases attack surface.\n+- **Adding `depends_on: someguy` to the `api` service:** While the API depends on Someguy, it already handles routing failures gracefully (DB fallback). A hard dependency would prevent the API from starting if Someguy is slow to initialize. Use a soft dependency or no dependency.\n+- **Changing resolution logic simultaneously with the URL swap:** Per CONTEXT.md, keep identical timeout/retry config for apples-to-apples comparison with Phase 18 baselines.\n+\n+## Don't Hand-Roll\n+\n+| Problem                          | Don't Build                              | Use Instead                        | Why                                                                                               |\n+| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| Delegated routing server         | Custom HTTP proxy to Kubo's DHT          | Someguy v0.11.1                    | Someguy IS the reference implementation. Maintained by IPFS Foundation. Same spec as delegated-ipfs.dev. |\n+| DHT client                       | Custom libp2p DHT node in Node.js        | Someguy's embedded Go DHT client   | Go implementation is battle-tested (same code as Kubo). JS DHT client has known reliability issues. |\n+| Health check endpoint for Someguy| Custom sidecar that polls Someguy        | Docker healthcheck on `/version`   | Simpler, built-in to Docker Compose, no extra container needed.                                    |\n+| Metrics for routing latency      | Custom timing wrapper around fetch       | prom-client Histogram + `observe()`| Existing pattern in MetricsService. Consistent with httpRequestDuration histogram.                 |\n+\n+**Key insight:** The entire Phase 19 infrastructure change is adding one Docker container and changing one environment variable. The `DelegatedRoutingClient` abstraction already exists -- don't bypass it.\n+\n+## Common Pitfalls\n+\n+### Pitfall 1: Someguy Default Listen Address is 127.0.0.1\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** Someguy defaults to `SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS=127.0.0.1:8190`. Inside a Docker container, `127.0.0.1` means \"only this container.\" The `api` container cannot reach it.\n+**Why it happens:** Someguy follows Go convention of binding to localhost by default for security.\n+**How to avoid:** Set `SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0:8190` in the Docker Compose environment.\n+**Warning signs:** `ECONNREFUSED` when `api` tries to connect to `http://someguy:8190`.\n+\n+### Pitfall 2: Accelerated DHT Mode Resource Consumption\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** Someguy with `SOMEGUY_DHT=accelerated` (the default) performs a full DHT crawl on startup, consuming 2GB+ memory and significant CPU/bandwidth for 12+ hours.\n+**Why it happens:** Accelerated mode builds a complete routing table for sub-second lookups -- great for public infrastructure, overkill for a single-app sidecar.\n+**How to avoid:** Set `SOMEGUY_DHT=standard` for staging. Consider `accelerated` only for production if standard mode latency is unacceptable.\n+**Warning signs:** Someguy container OOM-killed shortly after startup. High bandwidth usage from the VPS.\n+\n+### Pitfall 3: Someguy Has No /health Endpoint\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** Docker Compose health checks using `wget -qO- http://localhost:8190/health` fail with 404, causing container restarts.\n+**Why it happens:** Someguy only exposes `/routing/v1/*`, `/debug/metrics/prometheus`, and `/version`. No health endpoint.\n+**How to avoid:** Use `/version` for health checks: `wget -qO- http://localhost:8190/version || exit 1`.\n+**Warning signs:** Container stuck in \"starting\" state, repeated restarts in Docker logs.\n+\n+### Pitfall 4: Someguy Needs Outbound Internet Access for DHT\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** Someguy cannot resolve any IPNS names because it cannot connect to DHT bootstrap peers.\n+**Why it happens:** Docker network configuration or firewall blocks outbound connections from the `someguy` container.\n+**How to avoid:** Ensure the Docker network allows outbound connections (default bridge network does). Someguy uses libp2p ports 4001/udp + TCP for DHT. It does NOT need inbound ports -- DHT client mode works with outbound-only connections.\n+**Warning signs:** Someguy logs show \"failed to bootstrap\" or \"no peers found\". IPNS resolution returns 404 for known names.\n+\n+### Pitfall 5: Deploy-Staging Workflow Hardcodes DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** After updating `docker-compose.staging.yml`, the deploy workflow still writes `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=https://delegated-ipfs.dev` to `.env.staging`, overriding the intended Someguy URL.\n+**Why it happens:** The URL is hardcoded at line 377 of `deploy-staging.yml` in the `Generate .env.staging` step.\n+**How to avoid:** Change the hardcoded value to `http://someguy:8190` in the deploy workflow.\n+**Warning signs:** After deployment, API logs still show connections to `delegated-ipfs.dev` instead of `someguy:8190`.\n+\n+### Pitfall 6: Timing Metrics Not Instrumenting the Right Layer\n+\n+**What goes wrong:** Latency histograms placed at the controller level include HTTP overhead and serialization, making Someguy resolution look slower than it is. Or, placed only inside `DelegatedRoutingClient`, they miss DB fallback timing.\n+**Why it happens:** Unclear about which layer represents the \"IPNS resolution duration\" from the user's perspective.\n+**How to avoid:** Instrument at the `IpnsService.resolveRecord()` level for end-to-end resolution timing (includes network attempt + possible DB fallback). Add a second histogram at `DelegatedRoutingClient.resolve()` for pure Someguy latency. Both histograms together tell the full story.\n+**Warning signs:** Metrics show Someguy latency of 50ms but API p95 for `/ipns/resolve` is 2s -- the gap is the DB query + serialization overhead.\n+\n+## Code Examples\n+\n+Verified patterns from the existing codebase and official docs.\n+\n+### Docker Compose Someguy Service Definition\n+\n+```yaml\n+# Source: Someguy env vars docs + existing docker-compose.staging.yml patterns\n+someguy:\n+  image: ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1\n+  restart: unless-stopped\n+  environment:\n+    #         "body": "_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**Keep failed resolves out of `ipnsResolveDuration`.**\n\nLine 301 initializes `resolveFound` to `true`, and Line 372 only suppresses observation on the explicit `return null` path. If `findOne()`, `BigInt(...)`, or a non-`BAD_GATEWAY` resolve error throws after `startTime`, the `finally` block still records a latency sample even though nothing resolved. That will skew the new source histogram during outages. Initialize the flag to `false` and flip it only on successful non-null returns.\n\n\n<details>\n<summary>Suggested fix</summary>\n\n```diff\n-    let resolveFound = true;\n+    let resolveFound = false;\n...\n         if (dbSeq > networkSeq) {\n           this.logger.log(\n             `DB cache has newer sequence (${dbSeq} > ${networkSeq}) for ${ipnsName}, using DB: ${cached.latestCid}`\n           );\n           timerSource = 'db';\n           source = 'network_stale';\n+          resolveFound = true;\n           return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };\n         }\n         timerSource = 'network';\n+        resolveFound = true;\n         return result;\n       }\n\n       if (result) {\n         timerSource = 'network';\n+        resolveFound = true;\n         return result;\n       }\n\n       // Delegated routing returned null (404) or threw BAD_GATEWAY — try DB cache\n       if (cached?.latestCid) {\n         this.logger.log(`Resolved ${ipnsName} from DB cache: ${cached.latestCid}`);\n         timerSource = 'db';\n         source = 'db_cache';\n+        resolveFound = true;\n         return { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };\n       }\n```\n</details>\n\nAs per coding guidelines, `apps/api/**`: This is the NestJS backend API. Focus on: Proper error handling.\n\n\nAlso applies to: 365-365, 372-375\n\n<details>\n<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>\n\n```\nVerify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.\n\nIn `@apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts` around lines 299 - 301, The resolveFound\nflag is currently set to true and can cause ipnsResolveDuration to record\nlatency for failed/exceptional resolves; change the initialization of\nresolveFound to false and only set it to true at the point where a successful\nnon-null resolve is confirmed (after the database findOne() call and any\nBigInt(...) conversions succeed and just before returning the resolved value).\nUpdate the finally block to only call ipnsResolveDuration.observe(...) when\nresolveFound is true so thrown errors or early returns do not record latency;\nensure the same change is applied to the other mirror locations around lines\n365–375 where resolves and observations occur.\n```\n\n</details>\n\n<!-- fingerprinting:phantom:medusa:grasshopper -->\n\n<!-- This is an auto-generated reply by CodeRabbit -->\n\n✅ Addressed in commits 8505427 to 50365bb",

    40→   * Publish a pre-signed IPNS record to the IPFS network via delegated routing
    41→   * and track the folder in the database for TEE republishing
    42→   */
    43→  async publishRecord(
    44→    userId: string,
    45→    dto: PublishIpnsDto | PublishIpnsEntryDto,
    46→    recordType: 'folder' | 'file' = 'folder'
    47→  ): Promise<PublishIpnsResponseDto> {
    48→    const endTimer = this.metricsService.ipfsIpnsDuration.startTimer({
    49→      operation: 'publish',
    50→      source: '',
    51→    });
    52→    let result = 'success';
    53→
    54→    try {
    55→      // Validate base64 record
    56→      let recordBytes: Uint8Array;
    57→      try {
    58→        recordBytes = Uint8Array.from(atob(dto.record), (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));
    59→      } catch {
    60→        throw new BadRequestException('Invalid base64-encoded record');
    61→      }
    62→
    63→      // Save to DB first so resolve always has a fallback, even if delegated
    64→      // routing fails (e.g. rate-limited, network error, DHT propagation delay).
    65→      const folder = await this.upsertFolderIpns(
    66→        userId,
    67→        dto.ipnsName,
    68→        dto.metadataCid,
    69→        dto.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
    70→        dto.keyEpoch,
    71→        recordType,
    72→        dto.expectedSequenceNumber
    73→      );
    74→
    75→      // Publish to delegated routing API (best-effort — DB is the reliable source)
    76→      const publishStart = process.hrtime.bigint();
    77→      let publishOutcome = 'success';
    78→      try {
    79→        await this.delegatedRouting.publish(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes);
    80→      } catch (error) {
    81→        result = 'delegated_error';
    82→        publishOutcome =
    83→          error instanceof Error && error.name === 'AbortError' ? 'timeout' : 'error';
    84→        this.logger.warn(
    85→          `Delegated routing publish failed for ${dto.ipnsName}, DB record saved: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`
    86→        );
    87→      } finally {
    88→        const publishElapsed = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - publishStart) / 1e9;
    89→        this.metricsService.ipnsPublishDuration.observe(
    90→          { outcome: publishOutcome },
    91→          publishElapsed
    92→        );
    93→      }
    94→
    95→      return {
    96→        success: true,
    97→        ipnsName: dto.ipnsName,
    98→        sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
    99→      };
```
